15|
16|* Valid array: 19 x 8 over 6 symbols, every two rows agree in at most one column (equivalently no ordered symbol
17|  pair repeats in any two columns; equivalently a length-8 code over 6 symbols with minimum distance >= 7, i.e.
18|  A_6(8,7) >= 19). Contract: `run/screening/info-1-packing-array19-contract.json`.
19|* Lower bound 18: `data/cpro1-pa-18-8-6.txt` (CPro1), verified by `src/verify_pa.py` (row pairs) and
20|  `src/verify_pa2.c` (column pairs); mutation controls `controls/verifier-mutations/`.
21|* Upper bound 19: for 20 rows the least kappa per column is at (4,4,3,3,3,3), kappa 24; 8*24 = 192 > C(20,2) = 190.
22|* Corollary: NO valid 19-row array => maximum exactly 18. (Row deletion preserves validity.)

38|## 2. Row identity (used everywhere)
39|
40|For a valid array the rows agreeing with r in different columns are disjoint sets, so r agrees with exactly
41|A(r) = sum_c (n_{c,A[r][c]} - 1) <= 18 others and misses l(r) = 18 - A(r) rows; sum_r l(r) = 2L, 0 <= l(r) <= L.
42|With T(r) = P0 columns whose size-4 block (quad) contains r and w(r) = sum over special columns of e = block size - 3:
43|A(r) = 16 + |T(r)| + w(r), so |T(r)| + w(r) = 2 - l(r) in [2 - L, 2]. Necessary consequences:
44|* E0 (w = 0): t(r) <= 2 and l(r) = 2 - t(r) exactly.
45|* Quads of distinct P0 columns share <= 1 row; rows with identical nonempty T agree in every column of T.
46|* |T(r)| <= tmax = min(c0, 2 - wmin), wmin = sum over special columns X of (min block size of X) - 3.
47|* |T(r)| >= 3 forces w(r) <= -1, so r lies in a block of size <= 2 of some special column; hence the number of such
48|  rows <= hmax = total number of rows in size-<=2 blocks of the special columns (a union bound).
49|* One special column (c0 = 7): its multiset of e-values must be assignable to rows with 0 <= 2 - |T(r)| - e <= L.

164|## 8. Symmetry ledger: WLOG steps and simultaneous compatibility
165|
166|Global group G = S_19 (rows) x (S_6 wr S_8) (per-column symbol relabeling, column permutations). In every route
167|the order of normalisation is: (W1) permute columns so P0 columns come first and special columns follow the
168|canonical order; (W2) relabel symbols (quad -> 0; special labels by non-increasing count); (W3) permute rows and
169|P0 columns so the quad structure equals the listed labelled representative (graph G for E0, skeleton S for A2);
170|(W4, splits only) move a chosen P1 column to c* = c0 and map its (A,B,P) to the listed representative by an element
171|of Aut(S) x A/B swap x within-class row permutations, then A -> 0, B -> 1, P -> 5, triples -> 2..4; (W5) apply the
172|route's breaking. W1-W4 keep the case/subcase fixed; W5 must not move the array out of it.
173|* Lex-leader routes (M1 E0 encode_q0; M1 encode_skel; M1 encode_full/full2/partq/partbatch; M2 build_v2): all
174|  breaking constraints are A <= g(A) in ONE row-major order (rows top-down, columns left-right, cells compared by
175|  integer value) for generators g of the stabilizer H of the fixed structure: adjacent equal-count symbol swaps in a
176|  column (value precedence: first occurrence of s before s+1), transpositions of rows with identical fixed pattern
177|  (row r <= row r' lexicographically, columns left to right), transpositions of consecutive identical free special
178|  columns (column c <= column c+1 top-down). The lex-least element of the H-orbit of any canonical array satisfies
179|  every such constraint at once and still has the fixed structure (H preserves it).
180|* M2 v1 routes (build / build_part / build_partbatch): P0 triple-label FIXING per column (rows of Q_d \ Q_c,
181|  d maximizing |Q_d \ Q_c|, ties smallest d, get labels 1,2,3(,4) in row order) is a per-column relabeling applied
182|  after rows are fixed; the remaining breaking (first-occurrence among c* triple labels, equal-count labels of free
183|  special columns, top-down lex of consecutive identical free special columns) is lex-leader for a group acting only
184|  on special-column cells and not moving rows; no row lex. Hence both hold simultaneously.
185|* E0 M2 encode_q0b: only the per-column fixing (no lex constraints).
186|* Aut(G), Aut(S) and the choice among tied columns are left unbroken (cost, not soundness).

189|
190|* M1 at the pin: every counted case or placement index is backed by >= 1 drat-trim-verified UNSAT record; no M1
191|  placement list is empty (PA19FinalReceiptAudit: smallest M1 num_full 41). Split routes additionally rest on
192|  enumerator completeness (structural reduction, not proof-checked).

195|  EMPTY list (`cycle2/m2part_drat/ms09_b00003/ext.json` sha 2fd71b8e..., num_placements 0). Parent microproof
196|  `review/pa19-ms09-b3-structural-parent-v1.md` (sha f311d68d..., 01:46:28; copy in snapshot-supp2/): 5*21 + 3*22 =
197|  171 so every pair agrees exactly once; t(r) + e_1 + e_2 + e_3 = 2; the universal row h (t = 5) needs e_j = -1 in
198|  all three P1 columns; every other nonempty row shares a P0 quad with h; an empty row (t = 0) can never be in a
199|  pair (-1 + 1 + 1 < 2); so h has no partner. Count it as STRUCTURAL; it never inherits a solver/checker/proof hash
200|  or "s VERIFIED". aggregate15 would accept it vacuously (0/0); a different empty list is NOT accepted by this note.

1|# PA(19,8,6) lane (info-1, six-lane-2026-10-16)
2|
3|Frozen 2026-10-17T03:10-07:00 (host clock) BEFORE any search. Contract (read-only):

5|Owner of this directory only. No edits elsewhere.
6|
7|## Target (unchanged)
8|Decide whether a 19x8 array over {0,...,5} exists in which every pair of rows agrees in at
9|most one column (equivalently every N x 2 subarray has no repeated ordered pair; A_6(8,7) >= 19).
10|18 is known (CPro1 `result-18-8-6-seed1000.txt`); 19 is the counting upper bound.
11|
12|## Priority kill check (done 03:00-03:10, <=15 min)
13|* Colbourn-Ling 1999 and Cao-Du 2004 primary PDFs: NOT accessed. ScienceDirect and CORE
14|  (core.ac.uk/download/pdf/82008246.pdf) return captcha/Cloudflare challenges; not bypassed.
15|  Non-PDF challenge page retired to `archive/core-82008246-cloudflare-challenge.html`.
16|* zbMATH Zbl 0929.05013 (review of Colbourn-Ling): KSP design = maximum admissible number of
17|  parallel classes of triples plus possibly one block of size 2 or 4; resolves v = 4 (mod 6)
18|  completely and "nearly completely" v = 1 (mod 6). zbMATH Zbl 1064.05039 (Cao-Du summary):
19|  "improve the existence results on KPD({3,s*},v) for s=4,5". Neither summary names v=19.
20|* Secondary source with full statement: Su-Wang, Ars Combin. 91 (2009) 219-230,
21|  `sources/ars91-kpd-345star.pdf` (sha256 a314e0e6...8a7, OCR in `sources/ocr/`), Theorem 1.3:
22|  "(Cerny et al., Phillips et al., Colbourn and Ling, Cao and Du) There is a KPD({3,4*},v)
23|  containing floor((v-3)/2) parallel classes for every v = 1 (mod 3) with v > 25."
24|* Relation: a KPD({3,4*},19) with 8 classes is exactly a PA(19,8,6) whose 8 columns all have
25|  profile (4,3,3,3,3,3). The summarized theorem does not cover v = 19. No published v=19
26|  construction was found. This is an access-limited check: it does NOT establish open status,
27|  nonexistence, novelty or priority.
28|
29|## Exact representation and lemmas (frozen)
30|Array A: rows R = {0..18}, columns C = {0..7}, symbols S = {0..5}. Valid iff for all r<r',
31||{c : A[r][c] = A[r'][c]}| <= 1.
32|
33|L1 (counting). For column c with symbol counts n_{c,s}, kappa_c = sum_s C(n_{c,s},2) is the
34|number of row pairs agreeing in c. Validity => sum_c kappa_c <= C(19,2) = 171. By convexity
35|min kappa_c = 21 at counts (4,3,3,3,3,3). Hence total excess sum_c (kappa_c - 21) <= 3, and the
36|number of row pairs agreeing nowhere is exactly 3 - (total excess).
37|
38|L2 (profiles). Column profiles = partitions of 19 into <= 6 parts with kappa <= 24. Enumerated
39|by `src/profiles.py` (exhaustive over all partitions); expected list:
40|P0=(4,3,3,3,3,3) e0; P1=(4,4,3,3,3,2) e1; P2a=(5,3,3,3,3,2), P2b=(4,4,4,3,2,2) e2;
41|P3a=(4,4,4,3,3,1), P3b=(5,4,3,3,2,2) e3. All have exactly 6 nonzero parts.
42|Profile multisets over 8 columns with total excess <= 3 (also enumerated by the script):
43|E0 {P0^8}; E1 {P1,P0^7}; E2a {P2a,P0^7}; E2a' {P2b,P0^7}; E2b {P1^2,P0^6}; E3a {P3a,P0^7};
44|E3a' {P3b,P0^7}; E3b {P2a,P1,P0^6}; E3b' {P2b,P1,P0^6}; E3c {P1^3,P0^5}. ALL are searched.
45|No case is dropped; balanced-only results are never reported as nonexistence.

88|## Amendment A1 (recorded 04:04; encoder written ~03:53, first run 03:55): E0 quad-graph split
89|M1 M-class cases proved too slow (UNKNOWN at 600-900 s each; see checkpoints.md), so the
90|balanced multiset E0 is searched by a finer, complete split. Lemma (E0 only): let t(r) be
91|the number of columns in which row r lies in the size-4 block (quad). Row r meets
92|sum_c (n_{c,A[r][c]}-1) = 16 + t(r) <= 18 other rows, so t(r) <= 2 and the leave degree is
93|2 - t(r). Two quads share <= 1 row (else two rows agree twice). With n_i rows of t = i:
94|n_1 + 2 n_2 = 32, n_0+n_1+n_2 = 19, 2 n_0 + n_1 = 2*3 (leave has 3 edges), so n_2 = 13 + n_0,
95|n_0 <= 3. Hence the quads are the dual of a simple graph G on the 8 columns: rows in two quads
96|= edges, max degree <= 4, |E(G)| = 13..16, plus 4-deg(a) private rows per column and |E|-13
97|rows in no quad; G determines the array's quad cells up to row/column/symbol renaming.
98|All such G: `geng -q 8 13:16 -D4` = 486 graphs (309/136/35/6 for 13/14/15/16 edges),
99|`data/quadgraphs-8v-13to16e-D4.g6` (sha256 687602738d82...56d8c); independently reproduced as
100|a bijection by `src/graphs_check.py` (networkx atlas extension) in
101|`data/quadgraphs-independent-check.json`. Per G (`src/encode_q0.py`): quad cells fixed, five
102|triple symbols with count 3 per column, AMO agreements, per-row leave count exactly 2-t(r)
103|(<= on each row plus <= 3 total), value precedence of symbols 1..5 per column and lex order
104|between consecutive rows with identical quad membership. Both breaking families are
105|lex-leader constraints for the same row-major reading order, so every orbit's lex-least
106|array satisfies all of them. Independent replication M2' (`src/encode_q0b.py`, no shared
107|code, no auxiliary variables, column-pair 4-clauses, binomial counts, different symmetry
108|breaking: in column c the rows of Q_d minus Q_c (d maximizing that set) get labels 1,2,3(,4))
109|solved by CaDiCaL 3.0.1 with direct LRAT output. E0 results are RESTRICTED to all-balanced
110|columns; they say nothing about the other 9 multisets.

112|## Amendment A2 (recorded 04:11; generator written 04:06, first sample run 04:10): P0-quad
113|## skeleton split for the 9 non-balanced multisets E1..E3c
114|Facts (all from validity, any multiset): every P0 column has one quad; two quads of
115|different columns share <= 1 row. For row r let T(r) = set of P0 columns whose quad contains
116|r and w(r) = sum over special (non-P0) columns of (block size - 3). Row r meets
117|16 + |T(r)| + w(r) <= 18 rows, so |T(r)| <= 2 - w(r) and its leave degree is
118|l(r) = 2 - |T(r)| - w(r) in [0, L] (L = 3 - excess = number of row pairs agreeing nowhere).
119|Hence |T(r)| <= tmax = 2 - sum_X(min block size - 3), and a row with |T(r)| >= 3 has
120|w(r) <= -1, i.e. lies in a block of size <= 2 of a special column, so the number of such
121|rows is <= hmax = number of rows in size-1/2 blocks of the special columns. With a single
122|special column the multiset of its values e = size-3 must be assignable to rows with
123|0 <= 2 - |T(r)| - e <= L (exact necessary condition, checked by interval matching).
124|Skeleton = P0 columns as vertices, |T|=2 rows as edges of a simple graph (max degree 4),
125||T|>=3 rows as hyperedges, 4-deg private rows per vertex, remaining rows empty.
126|`src/gen_skel.py` enumerates all of them from `geng -q c0 -D4` (all graphs on c0 vertices)
127|plus every set of <= hmax pairwise-linear hyperedges of sizes 3..tmax respecting degree 4,
128|drops those needing more than 19 rows or failing the single-column filter, and deduplicates
129|colored incidence graphs with networkx isomorphism. Counts (skeletons/*.json):

136|special columns; all lex-leader for one row-major order (P0 columns first). Aut(skeleton)
137|unbroken. The skeleton generator has no independent replication yet. 181|1. Fix an A2 case: P0 skeleton S, rows labeled as in `encode_skel.rows_of`. In any valid
182|   array of this case each special column c has two size-4 blocks A_c, B_c, one size-2 block

184|   disjoint; two rows that already agree (common P0 quad or common earlier special block)

188|2. `src/extend_p1.py` enumerates all (A_c, B_c, P_c) satisfying 1, choosing within each
189|   class of rows with identical history (same P0 set T(r) and same roles so far) only the

192|   A_c, B_c are enumerated; results are deduplicated by isomorphism of colored incidence
193|   graphs (P0 quads, special blocks tied to their column, rows), which only merges placements
194|   related by Aut(S), A/B swaps or permutations of identical P1 columns.

196|   (P1 column: A -> 0, B -> 1, P -> 5, triples 2..4; P0: quad -> 0, triples 1..5), each row's
197|   leave degree l(r) = 2 - |T(r)| - w(r) is known; constraints: exactly-one cells, triple
198|   counts 3, AMO agreements, row r misses <= l(r) rows and <= L pairs miss overall (equality
199|   by counting), value precedence among triple symbols of each column, lex(row i) <=
200|   lex(row j) for rows with identical fixed pattern. Breaking constraints are lex-leader for
201|   one row-major order, so every valid completion has an image satisfying all of them.
202|4. Verdicts: a full skeleton is decided only by an UNSAT answer whose proof is checked
203|   online (kissat binary DRAT streamed into drat-trim -i, its LRAT streamed into lrat-check;
204|   stream SHA-256 recorded) or by a SAT model verified by both verifiers. Timeouts/errors
205|   stay UNKNOWN. An A2 case is UNSAT only if all of its full skeletons are.

===== FILE lane/src/profiles.py (sha256 a45c95175b9c7f0c28e9b4cc076a6e88ead4df777242483ebdb6496243ec02ef, 59 lines; shown 1-59) =====
1|#!/usr/bin/env python3
2|"""Exhaustive column-profile and profile-multiset enumeration for PA(N,k,q).
3|
4|A column of an N x k array over q symbols is a partition of N rows into at most q
5|symbol classes. kappa(profile) = sum C(n_s, 2) = number of row pairs agreeing there.
6|Validity (rows agree in <= 1 column) forces sum_c kappa_c <= C(N,2).
7|"""
8|import itertools
9|import json
10|import sys
11|
12|
13|def partitions(n, parts, maxpart=None):
14|    """All non-increasing tuples of length `parts` (zeros allowed) summing to n."""
15|    if maxpart is None:
16|        maxpart = n
17|    if parts == 0:
18|        if n == 0:
19|            yield ()
20|        return
21|    for first in range(min(n, maxpart), -1, -1):
22|        if first * parts < n:
23|            break
24|        for rest in partitions(n - first, parts - 1, first):
25|            yield (first,) + rest
26|
27|
28|def kappa(p):
29|    return sum(x * (x - 1) // 2 for x in p)
30|
31|
32|def enumerate_profiles(N, k, q):
33|    pairs = N * (N - 1) // 2
34|    allp = list(partitions(N, q))
35|    kmin = min(kappa(p) for p in allp)
36|    budget = pairs - k * kmin
37|    profs = sorted((p for p in allp if kappa(p) - kmin <= budget),
38|                   key=lambda p: (kappa(p), [-x for x in p]))
39|    multisets = []
40|    if budget >= 0:
41|        for combo in itertools.combinations_with_replacement(range(len(profs)), k):
42|            ex = sum(kappa(profs[i]) - kmin for i in combo)
43|            if ex <= budget:
44|                # sort positions by excess non-increasing (normalization step 1)
45|                ordered = sorted(combo, key=lambda i: (-(kappa(profs[i]) - kmin), i))
46|                multisets.append({"profiles": [list(profs[i]) for i in ordered],
47|                                  "excess": ex,
48|                                  "zero_agreement_pairs": budget - ex})
49|    return {"N": N, "k": k, "q": q, "row_pairs": pairs, "kappa_min": kmin,
50|            "budget": budget, "num_partitions_checked": len(allp),
51|            "profiles": [{"profile": list(p), "kappa": kappa(p),
52|                          "excess": kappa(p) - kmin} for p in profs],
53|            "multisets": multisets}
54|
55|
56|if __name__ == "__main__":
57|    N, k, q = (int(a) for a in sys.argv[1:4]) if len(sys.argv) >= 4 else (19, 8, 6)
58|    print(json.dumps(enumerate_profiles(N, k, q), indent=1))
59|

===== FILE lane/src/gen_skel.py (sha256 7042249df25c51f5deb3da7d560b8935b468d46798464e54cc5e0862bd74fafd, 146 lines; shown 1-146) =====
1|#!/usr/bin/env python3
2|"""P0-quad skeleton split for the non-balanced multisets E1..E3c (Amendment A2).
3|
4|For a multiset with c0 P0 columns and special (non-P0) columns X, row r lies in the quads of
5|a set T(r) of P0 columns. Facts (README A2): each P0 quad has 4 rows; two quads share <= 1
6|row; |T(r)| <= 2 - w(r) with w(r) = sum_{c in X} (n_{c,A[r][c]} - 3) >= wmin = sum_X min e;
7|rows with |T(r)| >= 3 have w(r) <= -1 so lie in a light block (size <= 2) of some special
8|column: their number is <= hmax = number of rows in light blocks of X.
9|Skeleton = hypergraph (simple graph of |T|=2 rows + hyperedges of size 3..tmax), private
10|rows (|T|=1) fill every P0 column to degree 4, the rest are rows with T empty. Enumerated
11|from geng graphs on c0 vertices (max degree 4) plus all hyperedge sets, deduplicated up to
12|isomorphism with networkx (colored incidence graphs).
13|Single special column (c0 = 7): exact feasibility filter: the special column's multiset of
14|e-values must be assignable to rows with 0 <= 2 - |T(r)| - e <= L (bipartite check).
15|Usage (research venv): gen_skel.py MULTISET_INDEX OUTDIR
16|"""
17|import itertools
18|import json
19|import os
20|import subprocess
21|import sys
22|
23|import networkx as nx
24|
25|HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
26|sys.path.insert(0, HERE)
27|import profiles  # noqa: E402
28|
29|def feasible_single(types_sizes, evals, L, B):
30|    # rows sorted by |T|; e-values must satisfy B-|T|-L <= e <= B-|T| ; greedy matching on
31|    # intervals (each row is an interval of allowed e); classic interval assignment.
32|    rows = sorted(((B - t - L, B - t) for t in types_sizes), key=lambda iv: (iv[1], iv[0]))
33|    pool = sorted(evals)
34|    used = [False] * len(pool)
35|    for lo, hi in rows:
36|        pick = None
37|        for i, e in enumerate(pool):
38|            if not used[i] and lo <= e <= hi:
39|                pick = i
40|                break
41|        if pick is None:
42|            return False
43|        used[pick] = True
44|    return True
45|
46|
47|def main():
48|    mi, outdir = int(sys.argv[1]), sys.argv[2]
49|    N, k, q = (int(x) for x in sys.argv[3:6]) if len(sys.argv) >= 6 else (19, 8, 6)
50|    P0 = (4,) + (3,) * (q - 1)
51|    B = N - 1 - 2 * k  # row r meets 2k + |T(r)| + w(r) <= N-1 others
52|    assert sum(P0) == N and B >= 0
53|    P = profiles.enumerate_profiles(N, k, q)
54|    kmin = P["kappa_min"]
55|    ms = P["multisets"][mi]
56|    profs = [tuple(p) for p in ms["profiles"]]
57|    c0 = sum(1 for p in profs if p == P0)
58|    X = sorted([p for p in profs if p != P0],
59|               key=lambda p: (-(profiles.kappa(p) - kmin), [-x for x in p]))
60|    L = ms["zero_agreement_pairs"]
61|    wmin = sum(min(x - 3 for x in p) for p in X)
62|    tmax = min(c0, B - wmin)
63|    hmax = sum(sum(x for x in p if x <= 2) for p in X)
64|    evals = None
65|    if len(X) == 1:
66|        evals = [x - 3 for x in X[0] for _ in range(x)]
67|    graphs = subprocess.run(["/opt/homebrew/bin/geng", "-q", str(c0), "-D4"], capture_output=True,
68|                            text=True).stdout.split()
69|    reps = {}
70|    stats = {"graphs": len(graphs), "raw": 0, "after_rows": 0, "after_filter": 0}
71|    for g6 in graphs:
72|        G = nx.from_graph6_bytes(g6.encode())
73|        E = [tuple(sorted(e)) for e in G.edges()]
74|        deg0 = [G.degree(v) for v in range(c0)]
75|        covered = set(E)
76|        cands = []
77|        for s in range(3, tmax + 1):
78|            for S in itertools.combinations(range(c0), s):
79|                if all(deg0[v] < 4 for v in S) and not any(p in covered for p in itertools.combinations(S, 2)):
80|                    cands.append(S)
81|
82|        def rec(start, chosen, deg, cov):
83|            yield list(chosen)
84|            if len(chosen) >= hmax:
85|                return
86|            for i in range(start, len(cands)):
87|                S = cands[i]
88|                if any(deg[v] >= 4 for v in S):
89|                    continue
90|                prs = list(itertools.combinations(S, 2))
91|                if any(p in cov for p in prs):
92|                    continue
93|                for v in S:
94|                    deg[v] += 1
95|                cov2 = cov | set(prs)
96|                chosen.append(S)
97|                yield from rec(i + 1, chosen, deg, cov2)
98|                chosen.pop()
99|                for v in S:
100|                    deg[v] -= 1
101|
102|        for H in rec(0, [], deg0[:], set(covered)):
103|            stats["raw"] += 1
104|            deg = deg0[:]
105|            for S in H:
106|                for v in S:
107|                    deg[v] += 1
108|            priv = [4 - d for d in deg]
109|            nrows = len(E) + len(H) + sum(priv)
110|            empty = N - nrows
111|            if empty < 0:
112|                continue
113|            stats["after_rows"] += 1
114|            tsizes = [2] * len(E) + [len(S) for S in H] + [1] * sum(priv) + [0] * empty
115|            if evals is not None and not feasible_single(tsizes, evals, L, B):
116|                continue
117|            # leave lower bound: rows with |T| = t have l >= 2 - t - wmax(row); skip (SAT handles)
118|            stats["after_filter"] += 1
119|            Gi = nx.Graph()
120|            for v in range(c0):
121|                Gi.add_node(("v", v), kind="v")
122|            for j, e in enumerate(E):
123|                Gi.add_node(("e", j), kind="e2")
124|                Gi.add_edges_from((("e", j), ("v", v)) for v in e)
125|            for j, S in enumerate(H):
126|                Gi.add_node(("h", j), kind="h%d" % len(S))
127|                Gi.add_edges_from((("h", j), ("v", v)) for v in S)
128|            key = (len(E), tuple(sorted(len(S) for S in H)), tuple(sorted(deg)),
129|                   nx.weisfeiler_lehman_graph_hash(Gi, node_attr="kind", iterations=3))
130|            bucket = reps.setdefault(key, [])
131|            nm = lambda a, b: a["kind"] == b["kind"]  # noqa: E731
132|            if any(nx.is_isomorphic(Gi, R[0], node_match=nm) for R in bucket):
133|                continue
134|            bucket.append((Gi, {"edges": E, "hyper": [list(S) for S in H], "empty": empty}))
135|    skels = [d for b in reps.values() for _, d in b]
136|    os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)
137|    out = {"N": N, "k": k, "q": q, "multiset_index": mi, "profiles": [list(p) for p in profs], "c0": c0,
138|           "special": [list(p) for p in X], "L": L, "wmin": wmin, "tmax": tmax, "hmax": hmax,
139|           "stats": stats, "num_skeletons": len(skels), "skeletons": skels}
140|    json.dump(out, open(os.path.join(outdir, "skeletons-ms%02d.json" % mi), "w"))
141|    print(json.dumps({k: v for k, v in out.items() if k != "skeletons"}))
142|
143|
144|if __name__ == "__main__":
145|    main()
146|

===== FILE lane/src/gen_skel_b.py (sha256 15978e8ce7ed8dad6a3362f113cbb539f909d069f686402cb63eeb0de6a5d8ce, 134 lines; shown 1-134) =====
1|#!/usr/bin/env python3
2|"""Independent second generator (M2) for the A2 P0-quad skeletons; shares no code with
3|gen_skel.py beyond the profile list (recomputed here independently).
4|
5|Representation: a skeleton is a bicoloured graph, first class = rows lying in >= 2 P0
6|quads (degree 2..tmax), second class = the c0 P0 quads (degree <= 4); two quads share <= 1
7|row (genbg -Z1 on the second class). nauty genbg enumerates these up to isomorphism
8|preserving the two classes, for every number h of first-class vertices (0 <= h <= 2*c0).
9|Rows in exactly one quad (4 - degree per quad) and rows in no quad complete the 19 rows.
10|Filters (same necessary conditions as README A2, re-derived): rows in >= 3 quads lie in a
11|size-<=2 block of a special column, so their number is <= hmax; total rows <= 19; with one
12|special column its multiset of e = size - 3 must be assignable to rows with
13|0 <= 2 - |T| - e <= L (checked here by brute-force bipartite matching).
14|Output: skeletons-b-msXX.json with each skeleton as a list of row types (sorted tuples).
15|Usage (research venv): gen_skel_b.py MS_INDEX OUTDIR
16|"""
17|import itertools
18|import json
19|import os
20|import subprocess
21|import sys
22|
23|import networkx as nx
24|
25|GENBG = "/opt/homebrew/bin/genbg"
26|
27|
28|def partitions19():
29|    out = []
30|
31|    def rec(n, k, mx, acc):
32|        if k == 0:
33|            if n == 0:
34|                out.append(tuple(acc))
35|            return
36|        for x in range(min(n, mx), -1, -1):
37|            rec(n - x, k - 1, x, acc + [x])
38|
39|    rec(19, 6, 19, [])
40|    return out
41|
42|
43|def kap(p):
44|    return sum(x * (x - 1) // 2 for x in p)
45|
46|
47|def multisets():
48|    parts = [p for p in partitions19()]
49|    kmin = min(kap(p) for p in parts)
50|    budget = 171 - 8 * kmin
51|    profs = [p for p in parts if kap(p) - kmin <= budget]
52|    profs.sort(key=lambda p: (kap(p), [-x for x in p]))
53|    res = []
54|    for combo in itertools.combinations_with_replacement(range(len(profs)), 8):
55|        ex = sum(kap(profs[i]) - kmin for i in combo)
56|        if ex <= budget:
57|            res.append(([profs[i] for i in combo], budget - ex))
58|    return res, kmin
59|
60|
61|def matchable(tsizes, evals, L):
62|    # bipartite matching rows -> e-values with 0 <= 2 - t - e <= L (Kuhn's algorithm)
63|    n = len(tsizes)
64|    adj = [[j for j, e in enumerate(evals) if 0 <= 2 - t - e <= L] for t in tsizes]
65|    match = [-1] * len(evals)
66|
67|    def aug(i, seen):
68|        for j in adj[i]:
69|            if not seen[j]:
70|                seen[j] = True
71|                if match[j] < 0 or aug(match[j], seen):
72|                    match[j] = i
73|                    return True
74|        return False
75|
76|    return all(aug(i, [False] * len(evals)) for i in range(n))
77|
78|
79|def main():
80|    mi, outdir = int(sys.argv[1]), sys.argv[2]
81|    ms, kmin = multisets()
82|    profs, L = ms[mi]
83|    P0 = (4, 3, 3, 3, 3, 3)
84|    c0 = sum(1 for p in profs if p == P0)
85|    X = [p for p in profs if p != P0]
86|    wmin = sum(min(x - 3 for x in p) for p in X)
87|    tmax = min(c0, 2 - wmin)
88|    hmax = sum(x for p in X for x in p if x <= 2)
89|    evals = [x - 3 for x in X[0] for _ in range(x)] if len(X) == 1 else None
90|    out = []
91|    counts = {}
92|    for h in range(0, 2 * c0 + 1):
93|        if h == 0:
94|            graphs = [None]
95|        else:
96|            cmd = [GENBG, "-q", "-Z1", "-d2:0", "-D%d:4" % tmax, str(h), str(c0)]
97|            graphs = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.split()
98|        kept = 0
99|        for g6 in graphs:
100|            if g6 is None:
101|                types = []
102|            else:
103|                G = nx.from_graph6_bytes(g6.encode())
104|                types = [tuple(sorted(v - h for v in G.neighbors(r))) for r in range(h)]
105|            deg = [0] * c0
106|            for t in types:
107|                for v in t:
108|                    deg[v] += 1
109|            if any(d > 4 for d in deg):
110|                continue
111|            big = sum(1 for t in types if len(t) >= 3)
112|            if big > hmax:
113|                continue
114|            nrows = len(types) + sum(4 - d for d in deg)
115|            if nrows > 19:
116|                continue
117|            empty = 19 - nrows
118|            tsizes = [len(t) for t in types] + [1] * sum(4 - d for d in deg) + [0] * empty
119|            if evals is not None and not matchable(tsizes, evals, L):
120|                continue
121|            out.append({"types": sorted(types), "empty": empty})
122|            kept += 1
123|        counts[h] = [len(graphs), kept]
124|    os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)
125|    json.dump({"multiset_index": mi, "profiles": [list(p) for p in profs], "c0": c0, "L": L,
126|               "tmax": tmax, "hmax": hmax, "per_h": counts, "num_skeletons": len(out), "skeletons": out},
127|              open(os.path.join(outdir, "skeletons-b-ms%02d.json" % mi), "w"))
128|    print(json.dumps({"multiset_index": mi, "c0": c0, "L": L, "tmax": tmax, "hmax": hmax,
129|                      "num_skeletons": len(out)}))
130|
131|
132|if __name__ == "__main__":
133|    main()
134|

===== FILE lane/src/compare_skel.py (sha256 e84bf44b9399b52db49fcd5ba2fd6c93832d5c5cbd8c25dec5527f2f35d282aa, 66 lines; shown 1-66) =====
1|#!/usr/bin/env python3
2|"""Case-for-case comparison of the two A2 skeleton generators (gen_skel vs gen_skel_b):
3|each skeleton is turned into a bicoloured graph (P0 quads vs rows in >= 2 quads, plus the
4|number of rows in no quad as a graph attribute); the two lists must be in bijection under
5|isomorphism, and each list must be internally duplicate-free.
6|Usage (research venv): compare_skel.py MS_INDEX SKELDIR_A SKELDIR_B OUT_JSON
7|"""
8|import json
9|import os
10|import sys
11|
12|import networkx as nx
13|
14|
15|def graph(c0, types, empty):
16|    G = nx.Graph()
17|    G.graph["empty"] = empty
18|    for v in range(c0):
19|        G.add_node(("q", v), kind="q")
20|    for j, t in enumerate(types):
21|        G.add_node(("r", j), kind="r%d" % len(t))
22|        for v in t:
23|            G.add_edge(("r", j), ("q", v))
24|    return G
25|
26|
27|def key(G):
28|    return (G.graph["empty"], G.number_of_edges(), G.number_of_nodes(),
29|            nx.weisfeiler_lehman_graph_hash(G, node_attr="kind", iterations=4))
30|
31|
32|def main():
33|    mi, da, db, out = int(sys.argv[1]), sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3], sys.argv[4]
34|    A = json.load(open(os.path.join(da, "skeletons-ms%02d.json" % mi)))
35|    B = json.load(open(os.path.join(db, "skeletons-b-ms%02d.json" % mi)))
36|    assert [list(p) for p in A["profiles"]] == [list(p) for p in B["profiles"]] or \
37|        sorted(map(tuple, A["profiles"])) == sorted(map(tuple, B["profiles"]))
38|    c0 = A["c0"]
39|    ga = [graph(c0, [tuple(e) for e in s["edges"]] + [tuple(h) for h in s["hyper"]], s["empty"])
40|          for s in A["skeletons"]]
41|    gb = [graph(c0, [tuple(t) for t in s["types"]], s["empty"]) for s in B["skeletons"]]
42|    nm = lambda x, y: x["kind"] == y["kind"]  # noqa: E731
43|    buckets = {}
44|    for i, G in enumerate(gb):
45|        buckets.setdefault(key(G), []).append(i)
46|    hits_b = [0] * len(gb)
47|    hits_a = []
48|    mapping = []
49|    for G in ga:
50|        h = [i for i in buckets.get(key(G), []) if nx.is_isomorphic(G, gb[i], node_match=nm)]
51|        hits_a.append(len(h))
52|        mapping.append(h[0] if len(h) == 1 else None)
53|        for i in h:
54|            hits_b[i] += 1
55|    res = {"multiset_index": mi, "count_a": len(ga), "count_b": len(gb),
56|           "a_each_matched_once": all(x == 1 for x in hits_a),
57|           "b_each_matched_once": all(x == 1 for x in hits_b)}
58|    res["bijection"] = res["count_a"] == res["count_b"] and res["a_each_matched_once"] and res["b_each_matched_once"]
59|    res["a_to_b"] = mapping
60|    json.dump(res, open(out, "w"), indent=1)
61|    print(json.dumps({k: v for k, v in res.items() if k != "a_to_b"}))
62|
63|
64|if __name__ == "__main__":
65|    main()
66|

===== FILE lane/src/gen_skel_cases.py (sha256 5c77c1177ba9167d1e754fa01f5227048a1c1d1b933190932dc5982334a8e5ce, 34 lines; shown 1-34) =====
1|#!/usr/bin/env python3
2|"""Turn skeletons-msXX.json files into run_cases specs (kind 'skel').
3|Usage: gen_skel_cases.py SKELDIR CASEDIR MS_INDEX [MS_INDEX ...]
4|"""
5|import json
6|import os
7|import sys
8|
9|
10|def main():
11|    skeldir, casedir = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
12|    mis = [int(x) for x in sys.argv[3:]]
13|    os.makedirs(os.path.join(casedir, "specs"), exist_ok=True)
14|    idx_path = os.path.join(casedir, "index.json")
15|    index = json.load(open(idx_path)) if os.path.exists(idx_path) else {"cases": []}
16|    have = {c["tag"] for c in index["cases"]}
17|    for mi in mis:
18|        d = json.load(open(os.path.join(skeldir, "skeletons-ms%02d.json" % mi)))
19|        for si, sk in enumerate(d["skeletons"]):
20|            tag = "ms%02d_s%05d" % (mi, si)
21|            spec = {"kind": "skel", "N": d.get("N", 19), "k": d.get("k", 8), "q": d.get("q", 6),
22|                    "multiset_index": mi, "skel_index": si,
23|                    "c0": d["c0"], "special": d["special"], "L": d["L"], "skeleton": sk}
24|            json.dump(spec, open(os.path.join(casedir, "specs", tag + ".json"), "w"))
25|            if tag not in have:
26|                index["cases"].append({"tag": tag, "multiset": mi, "skel": si})
27|    index["num_cases"] = len(index["cases"])
28|    json.dump(index, open(idx_path, "w"), indent=1)
29|    print(index["num_cases"])
30|
31|
32|if __name__ == "__main__":
33|    main()

===== FILE lane/src/graphs_check.py (sha256 27e798ed8a7645967f38e14f8f64c8b417c940418a5d284f5ab82f24f45834c0, 65 lines; shown 1-65) =====
1|#!/usr/bin/env python3
2|"""Independent check of the geng list for the E0 split: all simple graphs on 8 vertices
3|with max degree <= 4 and 13..16 edges, up to isomorphism, generated from the networkx
4|graph atlas (all graphs on 7 vertices) by adding an 8th vertex in every possible way,
5|deduplicated with nx.is_isomorphic. Then matched one-to-one against the geng file.
6|Usage (research venv python): graphs_check.py GENG_FILE OUT_JSON
7|"""
8|import itertools
9|import json
10|import sys
11|
12|import networkx as nx
13|
14|
15|def parse_g6(s):
16|    return nx.from_graph6_bytes(s.strip().encode())
17|
18|
19|def main():
20|    geng = [parse_g6(l) for l in open(sys.argv[1]) if l.strip()]
21|    reps = {}
22|    ncand = 0
23|    for H in nx.graph_atlas_g():
24|        if H.number_of_nodes() != 7 or (H.number_of_edges() and max(d for _, d in H.degree()) > 4):
25|            continue
26|        for k in range(0, 5):
27|            for S in itertools.combinations(range(7), k):
28|                if any(H.degree(v) + 1 > 4 for v in S):
29|                    continue
30|                m = H.number_of_edges() + k
31|                if not 13 <= m <= 16:
32|                    continue
33|                G = H.copy()
34|                G.add_node(7)
35|                G.add_edges_from((7, v) for v in S)
36|                ncand += 1
37|                key = (m, tuple(sorted(d for _, d in G.degree())),
38|                       nx.weisfeiler_lehman_graph_hash(G, iterations=3))
39|                bucket = reps.setdefault(key, [])
40|                if not any(nx.is_isomorphic(G, R) for R in bucket):
41|                    bucket.append(G)
42|    allreps = [R for b in reps.values() for R in b]
43|    by_edges = {}
44|    for R in allreps:
45|        by_edges[R.number_of_edges()] = by_edges.get(R.number_of_edges(), 0) + 1
46|    matched = [0] * len(geng)
47|    rep_hits = []
48|    for R in allreps:
49|        hits = [i for i, G in enumerate(geng) if G.number_of_edges() == R.number_of_edges()
50|                and nx.is_isomorphic(G, R)]
51|        rep_hits.append(len(hits))
52|        for i in hits:
53|            matched[i] += 1
54|    bij = (len(allreps) == len(geng) and all(x == 1 for x in matched)
55|           and all(h == 1 for h in rep_hits))
56|    out = {"candidates": ncand, "atlas_classes": len(allreps), "by_edges": by_edges,
57|           "geng_count": len(geng), "geng_each_matched_once": all(x == 1 for x in matched),
58|           "atlas_each_matched_once": all(h == 1 for h in rep_hits), "bijection": bij}
59|    json.dump(out, open(sys.argv[2], "w"), indent=1)
60|    print(json.dumps(out))
61|
62|
63|if __name__ == "__main__":
64|    main()
65|

===== FILE lane/src/encode_e1.py (sha256 2a76e8bdc813e92e60de4267bc0538eef0e0849effb692671fb044c14b16b830, 232 lines; shown 1-101) =====
[... lines 1-101 ...]
1|#!/usr/bin/env python3
2|"""Encoder E1 (method M1) for one normalized case of PA(N,k,q).
3|
4|Case = (column profile per position, M-class representative for positions 0,1).
5|See README "Symmetry reduction". Writes DIMACS CNF and a JSON variable map.
6|
7|Constraints (all implied by validity + normalization):
8|  EO   exactly one symbol per cell                      (x[r][c][s])
9|  AGR  y[r][r'][c] <- x[r][c][s] & x[r'][c][s];  AMO_c y[r][r'][c]
10|  CNT  column c >= 2 has exactly prof[c][s] rows with symbol s (sequential counters)
11|  FIX  columns 0,1 fixed to rows sorted by (a,b) over the 1-entries of M
12|  VP   columns >= 2: equal-count symbols s<s+1 in first-occurrence order
13|  LEX  consecutive positions >= 2 with identical profile: lex(col c) <= lex(col c+1)
14|  ROW  (pb) per row r: sum_c (n_{c,A[r][c]} - 1) <= N-1           [r meets each row <= once]
15|       and sum_c (n_{c,A[r][c]} - 1) >= N-1-L, L = number of row pairs with no agreement
16|       (= C(N,2) - sum_c kappa_c, fixed by the profile multiset)  [leave degree <= L]
17|  DUAL (dual) per column pair and ordered symbol pair: at most one row (redundant view)
18|"""
19|import json
20|import sys
21|
22|
23|class CNF:
24|    def __init__(self):
25|        self.nv = 0
26|        self.clauses = []
27|        self.names = {}
28|
29|    def var(self, name):
30|        v = self.names.get(name)
31|        if v is None:
32|            self.nv += 1
33|            v = self.nv
34|            self.names[name] = v
35|        return v
36|
37|    def new(self):
38|        self.nv += 1
39|        return self.nv
40|
41|    def add(self, cl):
42|        self.clauses.append(cl)
43|
44|    def at_most(self, lits, k):
45|        n = len(lits)
46|        if k >= n:
47|            return
48|        if k == 0:
49|            for l in lits:
50|                self.add([-l])
51|            return
52|        R = [[self.new() for _ in range(k)] for _ in range(n - 1)]  # R[i][j]: >= j+1 among 0..i
53|        self.add([-lits[0], R[0][0]])
54|        for j in range(1, k):
55|            self.add([-R[0][j]])
56|        for i in range(1, n - 1):
57|            self.add([-lits[i], R[i][0]])
58|            self.add([-R[i - 1][0], R[i][0]])
59|            for j in range(1, k):
60|                self.add([-lits[i], -R[i - 1][j - 1], R[i][j]])
61|                self.add([-R[i - 1][j], R[i][j]])
62|            self.add([-lits[i], -R[i - 1][k - 1]])
63|        self.add([-lits[n - 1], -R[n - 2][k - 1]])
64|
65|    def exactly(self, lits, k):
66|        self.at_most(lits, k)
67|        self.at_most([-l for l in lits], len(lits) - k)
68|
69|    def weighted_at_most(self, groups, cap):
70|        """groups: list over positions of [(literal, weight>=0), ...] with exactly one literal
71|        true per position (guaranteed elsewhere). Enforce sum of chosen weights <= cap.
72|        S[c][v] (v=1..cap) is forced true when the partial sum over positions 0..c is >= v."""
73|        prev = None
74|        for c, grp in enumerate(groups):
75|            cur = [None] + [self.new() for _ in range(cap)]
76|            for v in range(2, cap + 1):
77|                self.add([-cur[v], cur[v - 1]])
78|            if prev is not None:
79|                for v in range(1, cap + 1):
80|                    self.add([-prev[v], cur[v]])
81|            for lit, w in grp:
82|                if w > cap:
83|                    self.add([-lit])
84|                    continue
85|                if w >= 1:
86|                    self.add([-lit, cur[w]])
87|                if prev is not None and w >= 1:
88|                    for v in range(1, cap + 1):
89|                        if v + w > cap:
90|                            self.add([-prev[v], -lit])
91|                        else:
92|                            self.add([-prev[v], -lit, cur[v + w]])
93|            prev = cur
94|
95|    def write(self, path):
96|        with open(path, "w") as f:
97|            f.write("p cnf %d %d\n" % (self.nv, len(self.clauses)))
98|            for cl in self.clauses:
99|                f.write(" ".join(map(str, cl)) + " 0\n")
100|
101|

===== FILE lane/src/encode_q0.py (sha256 8d2840f4cc5116f8acbd96d5a9a9d1e432b660887a082cc969a94ee306419299, 139 lines; shown 1-139) =====
1|#!/usr/bin/env python3
2|"""Encoder Q0: balanced multiset E0 (all 8 columns (4,3,3,3,3,3)) split by quad graph G.
3|
4|Lemma (README "E0 quad-graph split"): in E0 every row lies in <= 2 quads (row budget), two
5|quads share <= 1 row, n2 - n0 = 13 and 2 n0 + n1 = 6, so the quads are the dual of a simple
6|graph G on the 8 columns with max degree <= 4 and 13..16 edges (geng 8 13:16 -D4).
7|Rows: one per edge {a,b} (in Q_a and Q_b), 4-deg(a) private rows per column a, n0=|E|-13
8|rows in no quad. Symbol 0 of column a = Q_a (fixed); symbols 1..5 = the five triples.
9|Symmetry breaking (lex-leader, row-major reading order): value precedence of symbols 1..5
10|in every column; lex(row i) <= lex(row j) for consecutive rows with identical quad
11|membership (private rows of one column; the no-quad rows).
12|Leave: row r has exactly 2 - q(r) rows it never agrees with (q(r) = #quads containing r).
13|"""
14|import json
15|import sys
16|
17|import encode_e1
18|
19|
20|def parse_graph6(g6):
21|    data = [ord(ch) - 63 for ch in g6.strip()]
22|    n = data[0]
23|    bits = []
24|    for d in data[1:]:
25|        for i in range(5, -1, -1):
26|            bits.append((d >> i) & 1)
27|    edges = []
28|    idx = 0
29|    for j in range(1, n):
30|        for i in range(j):
31|            if bits[idx]:
32|                edges.append((i, j))
33|            idx += 1
34|    return n, sorted(edges)
35|
36|
37|def skeleton(g6):
38|    n, edges = parse_graph6(g6)
39|    assert n == 8
40|    deg = [0] * n
41|    for a, b in edges:
42|        deg[a] += 1
43|        deg[b] += 1
44|    assert max(deg) <= 4 and 13 <= len(edges) <= 16
45|    rows = []  # list of frozenset of quad columns
46|    for a, b in edges:
47|        rows.append((a, b))
48|    for a in range(n):
49|        for _ in range(4 - deg[a]):
50|            rows.append((a,))
51|    for _ in range(len(edges) - 13):
52|        rows.append(())
53|    assert len(rows) == 19
54|    return n, edges, rows
55|
56|
57|def build(spec):
58|    n, edges, rows = skeleton(spec["graph6"])
59|    N, k, q = 19, 8, 6
60|    F = encode_e1.CNF()
61|    X = [[[F.var(("x", r, c, s)) for s in range(q)] for c in range(k)] for r in range(N)]
62|    for r in range(N):
63|        for c in range(k):
64|            F.add(list(X[r][c]))
65|            for s in range(q):
66|                for t in range(s + 1, q):
67|                    F.add([-X[r][c][s], -X[r][c][t]])
68|            F.add([X[r][c][0]] if c in rows[r] else [-X[r][c][0]])
69|    for c in range(k):
70|        for s in range(1, q):
71|            F.exactly([X[r][c][s] for r in range(N)], 3)
72|    M = {}
73|    for r in range(N):
74|        for r2 in range(r + 1, N):
75|            ys = []
76|            for c in range(k):
77|                y = F.new()
78|                ys.append(y)
79|                for s in range(q):
80|                    F.add([-X[r][c][s], -X[r2][c][s], y])
81|            for i in range(k):
82|                for j in range(i + 1, k):
83|                    F.add([-ys[i], -ys[j]])
84|            ms = []
85|            for c in range(k):
86|                for s in range(q):
87|                    m = F.new()
88|                    F.add([-m, X[r][c][s]])
89|                    F.add([-m, X[r2][c][s]])
90|                    ms.append(m)
91|            M[(r, r2)] = ms
92|    # leave: row r misses exactly 2 - q(r) rows
93|    Z = {}
94|    for (r, r2), ms in M.items():
95|        z = F.new()
96|        Z[(r, r2)] = z
97|        F.add([z] + ms)
98|    for r in range(N):
99|        zr = [Z[(min(r, r2), max(r, r2))] for r2 in range(N) if r2 != r]
100|        F.at_most(zr, 2 - len(rows[r]))
101|    F.at_most(list(Z.values()), 3)
102|    # value precedence on symbols 1..5
103|    for c in range(k):
104|        for s in range(1, q - 1):
105|            P = [F.new() for _ in range(N)]
106|            F.add([-P[0], X[0][c][s]])
107|            F.add([-X[0][c][s], P[0]])
108|            for r in range(1, N):
109|                F.add([-P[r - 1], P[r]])
110|                F.add([-X[r][c][s], P[r]])
111|                F.add([-P[r], P[r - 1], X[r][c][s]])
112|            F.add([-X[0][c][s + 1]])
113|            for r in range(1, N):
114|                F.add([-X[r][c][s + 1], P[r - 1]])
115|    # row lex among interchangeable rows (consecutive in the row order)
116|    nlex = 0
117|    for r in range(N - 1):
118|        r2 = r + 1
119|        if rows[r] != rows[r2] or len(rows[r]) == 2:
120|            continue
121|        nlex += 1
122|        E = [F.new() for _ in range(k - 1)]
123|        for c in range(k):
124|            guard = [] if c == 0 else [-E[c - 1]]
125|            for s in range(q):
126|                for t in range(s):
127|                    F.add(guard + [-X[r][c][s], -X[r2][c][t]])
128|            if c < k - 1:
129|                for s in range(q):
130|                    F.add(guard + [-X[r][c][s], -X[r2][c][s], E[c]])
131|    return F, X, {"edges": edges, "rows": [list(x) for x in rows], "row_lex_pairs": nlex}
132|
133|
134|if __name__ == "__main__":
135|    spec = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))
136|    F, X, info = build(spec)
137|    F.write(sys.argv[2])
138|    print(json.dumps({"vars": F.nv, "clauses": len(F.clauses), **info}))
139|

===== FILE lane/src/encode_q0b.py (sha256 3c431849be93bb198adf484e5dac144bd0ab56d86f541913bd9b17435586e24d, 120 lines; shown 1-120) =====
1|#!/usr/bin/env python3
2|"""Independent encoder Q0B (method M2) for the E0 quad-graph split; written separately
3|from encode_e1/encode_q0 and sharing no code with them.
4|
5|Same mathematical case (graph G from geng, quads fixed), different CNF:
6|  * cell variables v(r,c,s) = 1 + 48 r + 6 c + s;
7|  * column-pair definition: for columns c1<c2, symbols (a,b) and rows r<r' the 4-clause
8|    -v(r,c1,a) | -v(r,c2,b) | -v(r',c1,a) | -v(r',c2,b);
9|  * each triple symbol 1..5 at most 3 times per column by binomial 4-subset clauses (with
10|    15 non-quad rows and one symbol each this forces exactly 3);
11|  * leave (mathematical fact: a row in t quads agrees with exactly 16+t others, so it misses
12|    2-t rows): for each row pair not sharing a quad, a "miss" variable u or an agreement
13|    witness a(r,r',c,s) -> v(r,c,s) & v(r',c,s); u forced false if either row has t=2;
14|    per row at most 2-t misses and at most 3 misses in total, by binomial clauses;
15|  * symmetry: for each column c choose the other column d maximizing |Q_d minus Q_c| (ties:
16|    smallest d); those rows pairwise agree in d, so they lie in distinct triples of c and
17|    are labeled 1,2,3(,4) in increasing row order (pure relabeling of triples of column c).
18|  No row symmetry breaking and no per-row budget constraints.
19|"""
20|import itertools
21|import json
22|import sys
23|
24|
25|def g6_edges(g6):
26|    d = [ord(ch) - 63 for ch in g6.strip()]
27|    n = d[0]
28|    bits = [(x >> i) & 1 for x in d[1:] for i in range(5, -1, -1)]
29|    out, idx = [], 0
30|    for j in range(1, n):
31|        for i in range(j):
32|            if bits[idx]:
33|                out.append((i, j))
34|            idx += 1
35|    return n, out
36|
37|
38|def main():
39|    g6, out = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
40|    n, edges = g6_edges(g6)
41|    deg = [sum(1 for e in edges if a in e) for a in range(n)]
42|    member = []  # member[r] = set of quad columns of row r
43|    for a, b in sorted(edges):
44|        member.append({a, b})
45|    for a in range(n):
46|        member += [{a} for _ in range(4 - deg[a])]
47|    member += [set() for _ in range(len(edges) - 13)]
48|    assert len(member) == 19 and n == 8
49|    R, C, S = 19, 8, 6
50|    v = lambda r, c, s: 1 + 48 * r + 6 * c + s  # noqa: E731
51|    cl = []
52|    for r in range(R):
53|        for c in range(C):
54|            if c in member[r]:
55|                cl.append([v(r, c, 0)])
56|            else:
57|                cl.append([-v(r, c, 0)])
58|            cl.append([v(r, c, s) for s in range(S)])
59|            for s, t in itertools.combinations(range(S), 2):
60|                cl.append([-v(r, c, s), -v(r, c, t)])
61|    for c1, c2 in itertools.combinations(range(C), 2):
62|        for a in range(S):
63|            for b in range(S):
64|                for r, r2 in itertools.combinations(range(R), 2):
65|                    cl.append([-v(r, c1, a), -v(r, c2, b), -v(r2, c1, a), -v(r2, c2, b)])
66|    for c in range(C):
67|        for s in range(1, S):
68|            for quad in itertools.combinations(range(R), 4):
69|                cl.append([-v(r, c, s) for r in quad])
70|    fixed = {}
71|    for c in range(C):
72|        Qc = {r for r in range(R) if c in member[r]}
73|        best = None
74|        for d in range(C):
75|            if d == c:
76|                continue
77|            Qd = [r for r in range(R) if d in member[r] and r not in Qc]
78|            if best is None or len(Qd) > len(best[1]):
79|                best = (d, Qd)
80|        for lab, r in enumerate(sorted(best[1]), start=1):
81|            cl.append([v(r, c, lab)])
82|        fixed[c] = {"d": best[0], "rows": sorted(best[1])}
83|    # leave constraints (aux variables numbered after the cell variables)
84|    nv = R * C * S
85|    miss = {}
86|    for r, r2 in itertools.combinations(range(R), 2):
87|        if member[r] & member[r2]:
88|            continue  # already agree in a shared quad column
89|        wit = []
90|        for c in range(C):
91|            if c in member[r] or c in member[r2]:
92|                continue
93|            for s in range(1, S):
94|                nv += 1
95|                cl.append([-nv, v(r, c, s)])
96|                cl.append([-nv, v(r2, c, s)])
97|                wit.append(nv)
98|        if len(member[r]) == 2 or len(member[r2]) == 2:
99|            cl.append(wit)
100|        else:
101|            nv += 1
102|            miss[(r, r2)] = nv
103|            cl.append(wit + [nv])
104|    for r in range(R):
105|        mine = [u for (a, b), u in miss.items() if r in (a, b)]
106|        cap = 2 - len(member[r])
107|        for sub in itertools.combinations(mine, cap + 1):
108|            cl.append([-u for u in sub])
109|    for sub in itertools.combinations(sorted(miss.values()), 4):
110|        cl.append([-u for u in sub])
111|    with open(out, "w") as f:
112|        f.write("p cnf %d %d\n" % (nv, len(cl)))
113|        for c_ in cl:
114|            f.write(" ".join(map(str, c_)) + " 0\n")
115|    print(json.dumps({"vars": nv, "clauses": len(cl), "miss_vars": len(miss), "fixed": fixed}))
116|
117|
118|if __name__ == "__main__":
119|    main()
120|

===== FILE lane/src/encode_skel.py (sha256 dd871522a62191a90ee022758d852b1544f321f3f13b49fef1ebd31818a5cc22, 130 lines; shown 1-130) =====
1|#!/usr/bin/env python3
2|"""Encoder SKEL (Amendment A2): one P0-quad skeleton of a non-balanced multiset.
3|
4|Columns 0..c0-1 = P0 columns (skeleton vertices; symbol 0 = quad, fixed), columns c0..7 =
5|special columns (profile labels sorted by count, exact counts, free). Rows ordered: |T|=2
6|rows (skeleton edges), hyperedge rows, private rows by vertex, rows with T empty.
7|Constraints: EO cells; quad cells fixed; P0 triple symbols 1..5 exactly 3 each; special
8|column exact counts; AGR/AMO agreements; ROW budget sum_c (n-1) in [18-L, 18]; COVER at most
9|L row pairs with no agreement; value precedence (P0 symbols 1..5; equal-count special labels);
10|lex(row) for consecutive rows with identical T; lex(column) for consecutive special columns
11|with identical profile. All breaking constraints are lex-leader for one row-major order.
12|"""
13|import encode_e1
14|
15|
16|def rows_of(c0, skel, N=19):
17|    rows = [tuple(e) for e in skel["edges"]] + [tuple(h) for h in skel["hyper"]]
18|    deg = [0] * c0
19|    for t in rows:
20|        for v in t:
21|            deg[v] += 1
22|    for v in range(c0):
23|        rows += [(v,)] * (4 - deg[v])
24|    rows += [()] * skel["empty"]
25|    assert len(rows) == N, len(rows)
26|    return rows
27|
28|
29|def build(spec):
30|    c0, skel, special = spec["c0"], spec["skeleton"], [list(p) for p in spec["special"]]
31|    N, k, q = spec.get("N", 19), spec.get("k", 8), spec.get("q", 6)
32|    bq = spec.get("base_quad", True)  # False: 18-row control variant, base profile (3^q)
33|    profs = ([[4] + [3] * (q - 1)] if bq else [[3] * q]) * c0 + special
34|    assert len(profs) == k and sum(profs[0]) == N
35|    rows = rows_of(c0, skel, N) if bq else [()] * N
36|    F = encode_e1.CNF()
37|    X = [[[F.var(("x", r, c, s)) for s in range(q)] for c in range(k)] for r in range(N)]
38|    for r in range(N):
39|        for c in range(k):
40|            F.add(list(X[r][c]))
41|            for s in range(q):
42|                for t in range(s + 1, q):
43|                    F.add([-X[r][c][s], -X[r][c][t]])
44|            if c < c0 and bq:
45|                F.add([X[r][c][0]] if c in rows[r] else [-X[r][c][0]])
46|    for c in range(k):
47|        for s in range(q):
48|            if c < c0 and s == 0 and bq:
49|                continue
50|            F.exactly([X[r][c][s] for r in range(N)], profs[c][s])
51|    for r in range(N):
52|        for r2 in range(r + 1, N):
53|            ys = []
54|            for c in range(k):
55|                y = F.new()
56|                ys.append(y)
57|                for s in range(q):
58|                    F.add([-X[r][c][s], -X[r2][c][s], y])
59|            for i in range(k):
60|                for j in range(i + 1, k):
61|                    F.add([-ys[i], -ys[j]])
62|    kap = sum(n * (n - 1) // 2 for p in profs for n in p)
63|    L = N * (N - 1) // 2 - kap
64|    assert L == spec["L"]
65|    wmax = [max(p) - 1 for p in profs]
66|    for r in range(N):
67|        F.weighted_at_most([[(X[r][c][s], profs[c][s] - 1) for s in range(q)] for c in range(k)], N - 1)
68|        F.weighted_at_most([[(X[r][c][s], wmax[c] - (profs[c][s] - 1)) for s in range(q)]
69|                            for c in range(k)], sum(wmax) - (N - 1 - L))
70|    zs = []
71|    for r in range(N):
72|        for r2 in range(r + 1, N):
73|            z = F.new()
74|            zs.append(z)
75|            cl = [z]
76|            for c in range(k):
77|                for s in range(q):
78|                    m = F.new()
79|                    F.add([-m, X[r][c][s]])
80|                    F.add([-m, X[r2][c][s]])
81|                    cl.append(m)
82|            F.add(cl)
83|    F.at_most(zs, L)
84|    # value precedence
85|    for c in range(k):
86|        for s in range(q - 1):
87|            if c < c0 and s == 0 and bq:
88|                continue
89|            if profs[c][s] != profs[c][s + 1]:
90|                continue
91|            P = [F.new() for _ in range(N)]
92|            F.add([-P[0], X[0][c][s]])
93|            F.add([-X[0][c][s], P[0]])
94|            for r in range(1, N):
95|                F.add([-P[r - 1], P[r]])
96|                F.add([-X[r][c][s], P[r]])
97|                F.add([-P[r], P[r - 1], X[r][c][s]])
98|            F.add([-X[0][c][s + 1]])
99|            for r in range(1, N):
100|                F.add([-X[r][c][s + 1], P[r - 1]])
101|    # row lex for consecutive interchangeable rows (identical T, |T| <= 1)
102|    for r in range(N - 1):
103|        r2 = r + 1
104|        if rows[r] != rows[r2] or len(rows[r]) >= 2:
105|            continue
106|        E = [F.new() for _ in range(k - 1)]
107|        for c in range(k):
108|            guard = [] if c == 0 else [-E[c - 1]]
109|            for s in range(q):
110|                for t in range(s):
111|                    F.add(guard + [-X[r][c][s], -X[r2][c][t]])
112|            if c < k - 1:
113|                for s in range(q):
114|                    F.add(guard + [-X[r][c][s], -X[r2][c][s], E[c]])
115|    # column lex for consecutive identical special columns
116|    for c in range(c0, k - 1):
117|        c2 = c + 1
118|        if profs[c] != profs[c2]:
119|            continue
120|        E = [F.new() for _ in range(N - 1)]
121|        for r in range(N):
122|            guard = [] if r == 0 else [-E[r - 1]]
123|            for s in range(q):
124|                for t in range(s):
125|                    F.add(guard + [-X[r][c][s], -X[r][c2][t]])
126|            if r < N - 1:
127|                for s in range(q):
128|                    F.add(guard + [-X[r][c][s], -X[r][c2][s], E[r]])
129|    return F, X, {"rows": [list(t) for t in rows]}

1|#!/usr/bin/env python3
2|"""Independent encoder M2 for one A2 skeleton case, written separately from encode_skel.py
3|and the M1 helpers (no shared code). Input: a gen_skel_b skeleton (row types + #empty rows)
4|and the multiset's special profiles.
5|
6|Rows: the given types (rows in >= 2 P0 quads) in the listed order, then for each P0 column v
7|its 4 - deg(v) private rows, then the empty rows. Columns: P0 columns 0..c0-1, then the
8|special columns in the order given. Cell variables v(r,c,s) = 1 + 48 r + 6 c + s.
9|Clauses:
10|  * one symbol per cell (ALO + pairwise AMO);
11|  * P0 quad cells fixed (symbol 0 iff the row's type contains the column);
12|  * every symbol s of column c at most n_{c,s} times (binomial (n+1)-subset clauses; the
13|    counts sum to 19, so this forces exact counts);
14|  * column-pair definition: no ordered symbol pair twice in any two columns (4-clauses);
15|  * row identity (validity counting): with u(r,r') = 'rows r,r' agree nowhere' (forced true
16|    when no agreement witness a(r,r',c,s) -> v(r,c,s) & v(r',c,s) is chosen),
17|    sum_{r'} u(r,r') + sum_{special c} (n_{c,A[r][c]} - 2) = 2 - |T(r)| + #special,
18|    encoded exactly with a totalizer over the multiset of literals (size-1 blocks moved to
19|    the other side as negated literals);
20|  * symmetry: in each P0 column c the rows of Q_d minus Q_c (d maximizing that set) get triple
21|    labels 1,2,3(,4) in row order; in each special column, equal-count labels s < s+1 obey
22|    first-occurrence order (long clauses); identical consecutive special columns are
23|    lexicographically non-decreasing read top-down (order literals). The last two are
24|    lex-leader constraints for the column-major reading order of the special columns; the P0
25|    relabeling acts on other columns, so all hold simultaneously for some image of every
26|    valid array. No row symmetry breaking.
27|"""
28|import itertools
29|
30|
31|class Builder:
32|    def __init__(self, nfixed):
33|        self.nv = nfixed
34|        self.cl = []
35|
36|    def new(self):
37|        self.nv += 1
38|        return self.nv
39|
40|    def totalizer(self, lits):
41|        """unary sum: returns outputs o[0..n-1] with o[j] <-> (sum >= j+1)."""
42|        layer = [[l] for l in lits]
43|        if not layer:
44|            return []
45|        while len(layer) > 1:
46|            nxt = []
47|            for i in range(0, len(layer) - 1, 2):
48|                a, b = layer[i], layer[i + 1]
49|                o = [self.new() for _ in range(len(a) + len(b))]
50|                for x in range(len(a) + 1):
51|                    for y in range(len(b) + 1):
52|                        if x + y == 0:
53|                            continue
54|                        c = [o[x + y - 1]]
55|                        if x:
56|                            c.append(-a[x - 1])
57|                        if y:
58|                            c.append(-b[y - 1])
59|                        self.cl.append(c)
60|                for x in range(len(a) + 1):
61|                    for y in range(len(b) + 1):
62|                        if x + y >= len(a) + len(b):
63|                            continue
64|                        c = [-o[x + y]]
65|                        if x < len(a):
66|                            c.append(a[x])
67|                        if y < len(b):
68|                            c.append(b[y])
69|                        self.cl.append(c)
70|                nxt.append(o)
71|            if len(layer) % 2:
72|                nxt.append(layer[-1])
73|            layer = nxt
74|        return layer[0]
75|
76|
77|def build(c0, types, empty, special, N=19, K=8, Q=6):
78|    assert N == 3 * Q + 1
79|    deg = [0] * c0
80|    for t in types:
81|        for x in t:
82|            deg[x] += 1
83|    member = [set(t) for t in types]
84|    for x in range(c0):
85|        member += [{x} for _ in range(4 - deg[x])]
86|    member += [set() for _ in range(empty)]
87|    assert len(member) == N
88|    profs = [[4] + [3] * (Q - 1)] * c0 + [sorted(p, reverse=True) for p in special]
89|    assert len(profs) == K
90|    v = lambda r, c, s: 1 + K * Q * r + Q * c + s  # noqa: E731
91|    B = Builder(N * K * Q)
92|    cl = B.cl
93|    for r in range(N):
94|        for c in range(K):
95|            cl.append([v(r, c, s) for s in range(Q)])
96|            for s, t in itertools.combinations(range(Q), 2):
97|                cl.append([-v(r, c, s), -v(r, c, t)])
98|            if c < c0:
99|                cl.append([v(r, c, 0)] if c in member[r] else [-v(r, c, 0)])
100|    for c in range(K):
101|        for s in range(Q):
102|            if c < c0 and s == 0:
103|                continue
104|            n = profs[c][s]
105|            for sub in itertools.combinations(range(N), n + 1):
106|                cl.append([-v(r, c, s) for r in sub])
107|    for c1, c2 in itertools.combinations(range(K), 2):
108|        for a in range(Q):
109|            for b in range(Q):
110|                for r, r2 in itertools.combinations(range(N), 2):
111|                    cl.append([-v(r, c1, a), -v(r, c2, b), -v(r2, c1, a), -v(r2, c2, b)])
112|    U = {}
113|    for r, r2 in itertools.combinations(range(N), 2):
114|        u = B.new()
115|        U[(r, r2)] = u
116|        wit = []
117|        for c in range(K):
118|            for s in range(Q):
119|                a = B.new()
120|                cl.append([-a, v(r, c, s)])
121|                cl.append([-a, v(r2, c, s)])
122|                wit.append(a)
123|        cl.append(wit + [u])
124|    ns = K - c0
125|    for r in range(N):
126|        lits = [U[(min(r, x), max(r, x))] for x in range(N) if x != r]
127|        target = (N - 1) - 2 * c0 - ns - len(member[r])  # = 2 - |T| + ns when N=19, K=8
128|        for c in range(c0, K):
129|            for s in range(Q):
130|                n = profs[c][s]
131|                if n >= 2:
132|                    lits += [v(r, c, s)] * (n - 2)
133|                else:  # n == 1: coefficient -1 -> +(not v) on the left, +1 on the right
134|                    lits.append(-v(r, c, s))
135|                    target += 1
136|        o = B.totalizer(lits)
137|        if target < 0 or target > len(o):
138|            cl.append([])
139|            continue
140|        if target >= 1:
141|            cl.append([o[target - 1]])
142|        if target < len(o):
143|            cl.append([-o[target]])
144|    for c in range(c0):
145|        Qc = {r for r in range(N) if c in member[r]}
146|        best = None
147|        for d in range(c0):
148|            if d == c:
149|                continue
150|            S = [r for r in range(N) if d in member[r] and r not in Qc]
151|            if best is None or len(S) > len(best):
152|                best = S
153|        if best:
154|            for lab, r in enumerate(sorted(best), start=1):
155|                cl.append([v(r, c, lab)])
156|    for c in range(c0, K):
157|        for s in range(Q - 1):
158|            if profs[c][s] != profs[c][s + 1]:
159|                continue
160|            for r in range(N):
161|                cl.append([-v(r, c, s + 1)] + [v(r2, c, s) for r2 in range(r)])
162|    for c in range(c0, K - 1):
163|        c2 = c + 1
164|        if profs[c] != profs[c2]:
165|            continue
166|        eq_prev = None
167|        for r in range(N):
168|            for k in range(1, Q):
169|                # if all previous rows equal: value(r,c) >= k -> value(r,c2) >= k
170|                lhs = [v(r, c, s) for s in range(k, Q)]
171|                for s in lhs:
172|                    cl.append(([-eq_prev] if eq_prev else []) + [-s] + [v(r, c2, t) for t in range(k, Q)])
173|            if r < N - 1:
174|                e = B.new()
175|                for s in range(Q):
176|                    cl.append(([-eq_prev] if eq_prev else []) + [-v(r, c, s), -v(r, c2, s), e])
177|                eq_prev = e
178|    return B.nv, cl
179|
180|
181|def cnf_bytes(nv, cl):
182|    parts = ["p cnf %d %d\n" % (nv, len(cl))]
183|    parts.extend(" ".join(map(str, c)) + " 0\n" for c in cl)
184|    return "".join(parts).encode()

256|def build_part(c0, types, empty, special, placed, N=19, K=8, Q=6):
257|    """M2 subcase: like build(), with the blocks of the first len(placed) special columns
258|    fixed (A -> 0, B -> 1, P -> Q-1, the other rows of that column restricted to the triple
259|    labels 2..Q-2). Symmetry (column-major lex-leader on special columns): first-occurrence
260|    order among the triple labels of fixed columns and among equal-count labels of free
261|    columns; lexicographic order only between consecutive identical FREE special columns.
262|    The P0 triple-label fixing is unchanged."""
263|    # dedicated construction (the symmetry families differ from build())
264|    return _build_part_fresh(c0, types, empty, special, placed, N, K, Q)
265|
266|
267|def _build_part_fresh(c0, types, empty, special, placed, N, K, Q):
268|    deg = [0] * c0
269|    for t in types:
270|        for x in t:
271|            deg[x] += 1
272|    member = [set(t) for t in types]
273|    for x in range(c0):
274|        member += [{x} for _ in range(4 - deg[x])]
275|    member += [set() for _ in range(empty)]
276|    assert len(member) == N
277|    profs = [[4] + [3] * (Q - 1)] * c0 + [sorted(p, reverse=True) for p in special]
278|    assert all(p == [4, 4] + [3] * (Q - 3) + [2] for p in profs[c0:])
279|    m = len(placed)
280|    PAIR = Q - 1
281|    v = lambda r, c, s: 1 + K * Q * r + Q * c + s  # noqa: E731
282|    B = Builder(N * K * Q)
283|    cl = B.cl
284|    fixedcol = {}
285|    for i, (A, Bl, P) in enumerate(placed):
286|        c = c0 + i
287|        for r in A:
288|            fixedcol[(r, c)] = 0
289|        for r in Bl:
290|            fixedcol[(r, c)] = 1
291|        for r in P:
292|            fixedcol[(r, c)] = PAIR
293|    for r in range(N):
294|        for c in range(K):
295|            cl.append([v(r, c, s) for s in range(Q)])
296|            for s, t in itertools.combinations(range(Q), 2):
297|                cl.append([-v(r, c, s), -v(r, c, t)])
298|            if c < c0:
299|                cl.append([v(r, c, 0)] if c in member[r] else [-v(r, c, 0)])
300|            elif c < c0 + m:
301|                if (r, c) in fixedcol:
302|                    cl.append([v(r, c, fixedcol[(r, c)])])
303|                else:
304|                    for s in (0, 1, PAIR):
305|                        cl.append([-v(r, c, s)])
306|    for c in range(K):
307|        for s in range(Q):
308|            if c < c0 and s == 0:
309|                continue
310|            if c0 <= c < c0 + m and s in (0, 1, PAIR):
311|                continue
312|            n = profs[c][s]
313|            for sub in itertools.combinations(range(N), n + 1):
314|                cl.append([-v(r, c, s) for r in sub])
315|    for c1, c2 in itertools.combinations(range(K), 2):
316|        for a in range(Q):
317|            for b in range(Q):
318|                for r, r2 in itertools.combinations(range(N), 2):
319|                    cl.append([-v(r, c1, a), -v(r, c2, b), -v(r2, c1, a), -v(r2, c2, b)])
320|    U = {}
321|    for r, r2 in itertools.combinations(range(N), 2):
322|        u = B.new()
323|        U[(r, r2)] = u
324|        wit = []
325|        for c in range(K):
326|            for s in range(Q):
327|                a = B.new()
328|                cl.append([-a, v(r, c, s)])
329|                cl.append([-a, v(r2, c, s)])
330|                wit.append(a)
331|        cl.append(wit + [u])
332|    ns = K - c0
333|    for r in range(N):
334|        lits = [U[(min(r, x), max(r, x))] for x in range(N) if x != r]
335|        target = (N - 1) - 2 * c0 - ns - len(member[r])
336|        for c in range(c0, K):
337|            for s in range(Q):
338|                lits += [v(r, c, s)] * (profs[c][s] - 2)
339|        o = B.totalizer(lits)
340|        if target < 0 or target > len(o):
341|            cl.append([])
342|            continue
343|        if target >= 1:
344|            cl.append([o[target - 1]])
345|        if target < len(o):
346|            cl.append([-o[target]])
347|    for c in range(c0):
348|        Qc = {r for r in range(N) if c in member[r]}
349|        best = None
350|        for d in range(c0):
351|            if d == c:
352|                continue
353|            S_ = [r for r in range(N) if d in member[r] and r not in Qc]
354|            if best is None or len(S_) > len(best):
355|                best = S_
356|        if best:
357|            for lab, r in enumerate(sorted(best), start=1):
358|                cl.append([v(r, c, lab)])
359|    for c in range(c0, K):
360|        if c < c0 + m:
361|            prs = [(s, s + 1) for s in range(2, Q - 2)]
362|        else:
363|            prs = [(s, s + 1) for s in range(Q - 1) if profs[c][s] == profs[c][s + 1]]
364|        for s, s2 in prs:
365|            for r in range(N):
366|                cl.append([-v(r, c, s2)] + [v(r2, c, s) for r2 in range(r)])
367|    for c in range(c0 + m, K - 1):
368|        c2 = c + 1
369|        eq_prev = None
370|        for r in range(N):
371|            for k in range(1, Q):
372|                for s in range(k, Q):
373|                    cl.append(([-eq_prev] if eq_prev else []) + [-v(r, c, s)] + [v(r, c2, t) for t in range(k, Q)])
374|            if r < N - 1:
375|                e = B.new()
376|                for s in range(Q):
377|                    cl.append(([-eq_prev] if eq_prev else []) + [-v(r, c, s), -v(r, c2, s), e])
378|                eq_prev = e
379|    return B.nv, cl
